THE EXAMINER, FRIDAY, AUGUST 18, P• 99

EARLESTOWN & NEWTON.INEWTON URBAN DISTRICT COUNCIL. A meeting of this Council was held in the Town Hall, Earlestown on Monday. Mr. G. B. F. Borron, JP., C.C., presided. and there were also present : Messrs W Collingwood,J W Emmett, J.P., N Siddeley, W Gilmour, J Simpson, J N Patterson, J Sloe, W Thompson, J a Forster, E T Evans, T Orntand, with Mr Shirley (clerk), Mr Bowes (surveyor), and Mr Lloyd (gas engineer). GAS AND WATER COMMITTEE.

BOWLING.-BARLESTOWN V. CREWE.

This return match was played at Crewe on Saturday, and resulted in a victory for Earlestowia on both games of 2:3 points. After the match the players were taken through the Locomotive Works, and the various points explained to them.

At a meeting of this committee the following minutes of the Gasworks Extensions Committee were read as 1 follows, viz. :—The Gas Engineer reported that certain 'additions would be required to Messrs. Bryan, Donkin and Co.'s specificatioa for the new exhauster, and to Messrs. Dempster's specification for the new condensers; whereupon it was resolved that the local builders be in- vited to tender for the stonework required for the fcia- dations for the exhauster and condensers ; that an order be placed with Messrs. Bryan, Donkira ad Co. for four 12-inch pipe connections for the exhauster. that an iron girder, as per specification of the Gas Engineer, and 3,000 seconds bricks from Messrs. R. Evans and Co. for the foundations for the condensers,be obtained; that an order be placed with Messrs. Dempster and Sons for one 4-iach tar pot and connections to con- densers. The Clerk reported the result of his interview with Mr. Pardey as to the terms of a wayleave for a gas main from the gas works to Wargrave-road, viz. : Wayleave, £2 per annum; tenant to be compensated for damage to crops during laying of main; the main to be laid subject to rerneval by the Council after six months' notice at any time ; an agreement to be pre- pared by Lord Newton's solicitors at the expense of the Council. It was resolved that the Gas Committee bo recommended to accept the terms laid shown by Mr. Pardey. A letter dated June 20th, was read from Mr. Woods, veterinary surgeon,of Wigan, stating that be had had offered to him two herses suitable for the work of the Gas Committee at £63 each. It was resolved that Mr. Wooas be instructed to purchase same immediately if not disposed of, in which case the Clerk to instruct him to go as high as £6O each for two suitable horses; and that the wages of H Rylance, a youth engaged at the gasworks, be increased from 134 to 93 per week. With reference to a circular letter asking the Ceuncil to amalgamate with other Local authorities in forming a Mutual Insurance Society under the Workmen's Com- pens scion Act, it was reselved that tee Gas Committee be recommended to favourably eatertam the ratter at rates not exceeding 3s per cent. A letter was read front the Secretary to the General Post Offl e, London, referring to the increased consump- tion of gas at the Newton-le-Willows Post Office during the quarter ended 31st December last, and that they were of opinion that a further rebate, in addition to the 10 per cent. already allowed, should he merle therefrom. It was ordered that the Clerk reply stating that the matter was fully considered at the time and that they cannot see they way to make any further reduction. The Chairman submitted the report of Mr. Gsskin, the consulting engineer for the test borehole for a proposed town's supply of water. After the same had been read and discussed by the Com- mittes it was resolved that the matter be again referred to thelspecial water committee with power to obtain estimates of the complete scheme as laid down in Mr. Gaskia's report, and also to submit any alternative scheme which may appear to the special committee advisable, with an estimate of the cost thereof, and afterwards report to this Committee.—ln answer to Mr. Emmett. Mr. Ormand stated that the horses had been bought for £53 each. The minutes were passed, on the proposition of the Cbairman. HIGHWAYS AND SANITARY COMMITTEE. The Clerk reported to this committee, the result of the interview of the Chairman of the Ceuncil and himself and the County Surveyor, with reference to the amount of grant offered by the County Council, for special worka on the main roads during the year eliding 31st March, 1900, and that in consequence of the interview, the Lan- cashire County Council had offered an additional grant of £32 for half cost of flagging Church-street six feet wide, whereupen it was resolved that the offer of the County :Council b 3 accepted.—The following ing plan was approved and passed, viz : New classroom to Vulcan Infant School.—Letters , were read from the Bedford Brewing and Malt- ing Co., Leigh; the Earlestown Co-operative Society,and the Leaden and North-Western Railway Company, objecting to the proposals of the Council to the erection of urinals near the Oak Tree Inn, Nowton-le-Willows.and in Earle-street, Eariestown. The committee thereupon decided te postpone the whole question of public urinals. A letter WAS read from Messrs. J. Duckett and S on, Limited. Burnley, withdrawing their tender for drain and sewer pipes. &a., as they had made an error in their calculations, whereupon it was resolve 1 that the tender of the Turton Moor Sanitary Pipe Company. of Darwon, be accepted (in lieu of Messrs. Duckett and Sons tender). The Clerk reported (a) that, as instructed at the last meeting he had interviewied Mr. Pardey, and had endeavoured to obtain hone him a lease of the land for the proposed sits for treatment of nightsoil refuse off Vista-road for a period of years, but that Mr. Pardey could not see his way to accede thereto, and that he had interviewed Mr. Berry, the tenant of the land referred to, and that he (Mr. Berry) had obtained Mr. Pardey's permission to sub-let the land as yearly tenants to the Council, and that the rent would be £8 per annum, free of all rates and taxes. It was resolved that Mr. Berry's terms be accepted, sub- ject to an agreement being entered into. A letter was read from the Nledical Ofneer of Health, and stated that, as instructed, he had visited and inspected the Grand Stand on Newton Common Racecourse, and was of opin- ion that it was unsuitable for an isolation hospital. In pursuance of a letter from the Prestwich Urban Distriot Council, it was resolved that it is deairable that Urban District Councils shall be empowered by Act of Parlia- ment or otherwise to borrow money on security of local rates for purposes of private street improvement with. out being sompslled to obtain the sanction of the Local crovernment Board in that behalf ; and tbat a copy of this resolution be forwarded to Colonel R. Pilkington, M.P., for the Newton Divisios. The Clerk reported that he bad bean in communication with the Lancashire , County Council with reference to a resolution passed by the County Council in November, 1898, urging the Local Government Board to relax the rule at present obtaining, when applications for sanction to loans for private streets are being considered, which has the effect of making street formation dependent upon the efficiency of sewage works ; and that the County Coun- cil stated that the Board had replied that they are not prepared to make any variation in their present model of procedure. The Clerk further reported that ha had I also communicated directly with the Local Government Board upon the same subject, and read a letter from the Board dated the lith June, 1899, in which they stated that when they leern that the Sewage Disposal Works of the District Council have been put into proper condition they will be prepared further to consider the application of the CIURCiI for sanction to borrow for works of private street improvement. At a mooting of the committee held last Monday afternoon the Chairman reported that his 9oneur Judge Wynne Ffonlkes had given/a decision against the !Council in the case, of the Council v. Lyon who was summoned for the non-pay- ment of hawking tolls. After c snsideration by the com- mittee it was resolved that the Council be recommended to lodge an appeal against the decision of his honour. The minutes were approved. GENERAL PURPOSES COMMITTEE.

A letter was read before this Committee from Mr. ArthurlSmith, Wigan,solicitor to the Lancashire Electric Power Company stating that the Company proposed renewing their application for an Act in the next session of Parliament for powers for the supply of electric energy throughout the principal manufacturing parts of Lancashire, and that with a view of explaining the Bill he would esteem it a favour if the Council would grant an interview to a deputation from the Company. It was resolved that the matter be referred to the Light Railways Committee of the Council to consider the ques- tion fully and afterwards report. The Chairman moved the confirmation of the minutes which were agreed to. MR. GILMOUR SUGGESTS FOUR NEW MAGISTRATES.

In pursuance of notice of motion Mr. Gilmour moved " That the Council do now take into consideration the question of the desirability of recommending to the proper authorities, the appointment of additional county magistrates for this division." He said he would like it to be understood that he did not wish to give any offence to those sitting on the Bench at the present time. His resolution was intended to support them in their work if possible. In recent years the number of magistrates had been considerably reduced. They had lost by death Colonel McCorquodale, Mr. Pearson, and Mr. Mitchell, and of those remaining, four seldom attended. viz : Mr. Norman McCorquodale, Mr. A. C. McCorquodale, Mr. T. D. Grimke Drayton, and Mr. Gooch. The police experienced great difficulty in getting summonses signed. The police did not care to complain but they were put to great 'inconvenience in getting summonses signed. If M r. Borron and Mr. Emmett happened to be away there was practically no one tre be had to sign summonses. He was certain of this that no one could charge him of having anyone be- hind him. It was thought that he (Mr. Gilsnour) had something to do with the question asked in the House of Commons with reference to the Newton magistrates but he might say that he had no connection with the matter at all. He knew that it was usual for Justices of the Peace to be elected for political considerations but there was no consideration of politics in the question he was now bringing forward, and they as a Council ought to set an example in this matter. If their Chairman (Mr. Barron) vocated the position at the end of the present year, as he had already intimated. the Baugh would be without his services. Mr. Gilmour proceeded to suggest four members of the Council as being well- qualified to fill the position of magistrate, viz : Mr. Borren, Mr. F. J. Wood, Mr. Collingwood and Mr. T. Ormand. He proposed that they be recommended to the proper authorities as fit and proper persons to occupy a place on the Bench ef magistrates. Mr. Emmett did not think Mr. Gilmour was in a posi- tion to knew that the police had any difficulty in getting

summonses signed. He could say that no polieeman had come to his house to get a summons signed without getting what he wanted. The Chairman stated that last year the Ceiancil corn- rnittEd itself to a proposition asking the locil bench ol magistrates wuether it was net desirable that addi- tional magistrates should be appointed. A letter was received an i laid before the Council from Mr. Greenall, the clerk to the justices, stating that the appointment of additional magistrates has always had the careful consideration of the magistrates when the necessity required it. A question was put in the House of Commons by a member who did not represent thet divi- sion, but who was otherwise interested in it,wich refer- ence to the need of additional magistrates, and the Home Secretary replied that after considering the num- ber of magistrates there were sufficient for the district. For the information of the Council he (the Chairman) might state that there were 21 permanent magistrates. In addition that Council appointed one Golborne appointed anetner, and Haydock a third. In Newton there were three permanent magistrates, and iii Earle,- town one permanent magistrate. In Haydock and G3l- - there was no permanent magistrates. He thought that Council was obtruding itself by pressing forward any question of this kind, especially when adjoining districts had no permanent magistrates. These districts were represented on the Beech just as the Newton authority was by its Chairman of the Council. These three gentlemea by themselves were quite capable of carrying on the whole of the business ot the district independent of the permaneat magistrates. It was a pity to raise the question there because it was arrogat- ing to themselves a position they did not really hold. That Council was not responsible to the justices and tees, were in no way connected with the police. They were a health body deputed to look after the require- ments of the district and not to interfere in the criminal business of the district. If they interfered er pretended to assume a position in making recommendations for which they had no authority, they would lower the dignity of the Council. He thought the proper parties would, when they saw that the work was not done well, make additional appointments. There was no complaint as to the way the magistrates performed their duties,aud the statement about the difficulty of getting summonee signed was denied. It would be a mistake to pass the resolution suggested. It was not within their province to interfere in any way which might be looked upon as departing from the position they were placed in. Mr. Forster did not think it was necessary or cour- teous to the Belch to piss the resolution suggested. Tiaev woald probably be snubbed for doing 93. Mr. Siddelev seoended the proposition. Mr. elilmour said since the Councit was able to appoint a magistrate, it was a matter which could be brought before the Council. Vicars, engineers, Earlestown, was closing the doors of a wagon which he had bean assisting to uaload, when the door slipped and fell heavily on his right foot. PLUCKY RESCUE BT AN EARLESTOWN YOUTH.

Lily smart, a little girl aged 11, of Alfred-street, Newton-le-Willows, had a narrow escape from drowning on Tuesday morning about 10-30 a.m. She was playing with 801120 companions on the stone wall which runs along the Mill Brook, Newton-le- Willows on the side nearest the railway and it is supposed she must have slipped and fallsn into the water which at that point is very deep. Her screams and those of her companions draw the attention of a youth named James Brammar, of Wargrave-road, Earlestown, who was passing at the time. He immediately threw off his jacket and jumped into the water and after a while succeeded in bringing the girl to the side. He carried her home and Mrs. Smart being out, got some neigbours t 3 wrap the child in blankets. Dr. Watkins arrived soon after- wards and restoratives were applied, until con- sciousness returned. The girl seems none the worse now, as owing to the timely and plucky relcue of Brammar she ecapecl with nothing worse than a severe ducking. PIGEON FLYING.

HOPE OF EADLESTOWN LODGE, No 1644

A meeting of this Lodge was held in the Co-opera- tive Hall, Wargrave-road, on Monday, Bro Williams, C.T. presiding. After the opening services, the L.D. Bro. Woods read an invitation from the Hope of Sutton Lodge to pay them a visit during this quar- ter, which was accepted, the date to be fixed by the Programme Committee. The G.O T. circular and D.L. circular regarding the Twentieth Century Fund of £l5O for mission work in the district, was referred to them for consideration, and their report thereon. Bro Woods reported a Sister having been dangerously ill, but who was now convalescent. A Brother who had violated was restored to member- ship, and vote of condolence was passed to two Brethren whose children had died. It was resolved on a motion that they present an emblem to Bro Hobbs, who is leaving the town, with the thanks of the lodge for his past services. The election of officers for the next quarter was then proceeded with and the following were elected : C.T., Bra Francis ; V.T.' Sister Smith;' Secretary, Bro Singleton; F.Sec.,Bro Leatherbarrow ; Treas., Bro Warburton ; Chap., Sister Francis; Mar. Bro Macklin ; Guam, Bro Pope; Sentinel, Bra Leather- barrow, sen.; assistant secretary, Bro Mawsdley ; and D.M., Sister Grant. The reports of the retiring officers showed a membership of 105 in the lodge and 100 in the juvenile temple with a fair balance in the hands of the treasurer. The new officers ware then installed by Bro Woods, L.D., and after- wards Bro Francis thanked the lodge for the honour they had done him and hoped they would have a prosperous quarter. The following committees were;appointed:—Finance.Bros.Clarke and Williams; sick, Bros Newman and Dring, Sisters Grant and Owen ; programme, Bros Clarke, Willis, Woods, Sisters Francis, Smith and Grant. The lolg I was then closed by Bro Francis, C.T., at 10 o'clock.

EA.H.LESTOWN FOOTBALL CLUB.

PROSPECTS FOli, TIM COSIING SEASON.

The officials of our premier :local club have been busy during the closing summer months, gathering together a combination of players to do duty for nem during the ensuime football season. Having been promoted to the Lancashire League, they are well aware of the fact that a better standard of play will naturally be expected by their supporters, than was shown in the Alliance. Prac- tically all the players who rendered such yeomen service to the club last season have been re-engaged but nothing sensational bite been done in the secur- ing of new men. The last line of defence, the goal, will again be guarded by Chew. The backs will be selected from Heyes, Wilkinson, Witherington (Bamfurlong) and Davies (Earlestown Rovers). Four half-backs have signed viz.—Green, Parr, Lee and Pennington (Earlestown Rovers). All of last year's forwards have signed on except Ilarrissort whose injured knee troubled him a lot last season, The attack will therefore be in the charge of Smith, Linton (the Bamfurlong crack), Hampson, Ellison (Earlestown Rovers), Settle and Leach. Royle, a Manchester youth, will also be tried but this player's signature has not yet been obtained. The colours will be the same as last season; white jerseys with Maltese cross and blue knickers. A trial game will be played on the elesnes Ground on August 26th, between Earlestown and Earlestown Rovers, so as to get the men into form for their first league match, on September 2nd, when Dar- wen will be encountered also on the Mesnes Ground. During the last week or two, the officials have been getting the ground into something like order. The pavilion has been painted, and all the boards round the ground are t 3 be whitewashed. The only thing that remains wanting on the ground is a stand. If our local rivals, Haydock, can afford to build one, surely Earlestown can. We would sug- gest that a substantial one be built, and not one warranted to tumble down every time a gust of wind comes near;it. The supporters of Earlestown are confident that the club will make a good show, and expect them to finish somewhere about mid- way in the League. The geaeral regret is that Mr. G. W. Stringer has resigned the secretaryship, he having found that he could not cope with the duties, but a suitable substitute seems to have been found in Mr. A. Perks.

A RUNCORN BOY DROWNED OFF NORMA.N ROAD.

A DANGEROUS PH'.

About 2 o'clock on Thursday afternoon considerable sensation was caused by the circulation of the news that a boy hld been drowned in Wilson's clay pit, off Norman-road, Runcorn. Numbers of people went in that direction and soon discovered the news was only too true. It appears that a number of youngsters went to the pit with the intention of bathing,but only one of them, George Jones, aged 8 years (son of Paul and Mary Ann Jones, of 82, Shaw-street),eivested himself of his clothing and enterei the water. The pit, however, is some 12 feet deep at shallowest end, and 20 feet deep at the ether, and the result was the little child sank straight away. The ether children : Joseph Lyons,, Harry and Samuel Lvdiate, and Frank Ashley (all aged 7 years), then ran off and gave the alarm. 9 man named knthony Hockwell, employed at Jackson's cycle store, Dale-street, was first , on the scene, and tying two long ropes together succeeded in recovering the body at the first attempt. It was then lying in 12 feet of water. Being joined by Constable T. Spencer Bowyer, Dr. Sylvester's method of artificial respiration was tried for over half an hour but without success. Subsequently Dr.Ormsby appeared on the scene, and pronounced life extinct. The pit is considered a very dangerous spot, and one that should be more securely fenced off from the public.

Sr. HELENS ENGINEERS' RETURN FROM CAMP.

The 2nd V. 13, Lancashire Royal Engineer Volunteers returned home to St. Helens on Sunday afternoon after enjoying a splendid week's camp at Bare, Morecambe. The regimental carp was under the command of Colonel W. H. Pendlebury, VAC, and was pitched in an excellent, position adjacent to the seashore, where during the week the corps was exercised in a variety of en- gineering work. The camp was 420 strong, and through- out the week the hualth and dieeiplina of the men were exceptienally good. On Saturday the camp was inspected by Lieutenant-Colonel E. J. T. Ross, of Bladenburg, C.R.E. of Liverpool. Colonel Ross desired the commanding officer to convey to the officers and men the fact that he was extremely well pleised with the comp and everything he Saw, and especially to find that the conduct of the men had been so exceptional. On Sunday morning, the regiment were engaged dismantling the bridges and clearing up the camp, and at half-past nine Divine service was held,tho preacher being the Rev. J. C. Pigot. formerly of St. Pielens. About a quarter to three o'clock, the special train started from Bare, and landed the regiment at St. Helens about four o'clock where the men, who looked extremely well, were greeted by a large number cf friends. THE SUICIDE OF A ST. HELENS GIRL.

On Saturday, Mr. S. Brighouse, county coroner, held an inquest at St. Helens Town Hall on the body of Mar- garet Ann Lundy,23 years of age, a domestic servant in the employ of Mr. J. Broonthead, of Regent's-road, who was found drowned in the canal, near Raves-street on Thursday night. Annie Cattera.ll, of 140, Back Liver- pool-road,said she was with the girl Lundy on Thursday. Lundy told her that she had had "some words"witis her :young man, and intended to do away with herself. She afterwards stated, however, that she did n.)t mean it. —Maggie Fairhurst, of 86, Eidon.street, said she saw the deceased about a quarter-past tenon Thuisday night Lundy then said that if anybody would give her a knife she would cat her threat.—Peter Dwney,a young man, of 109, Napier-street, a collier, said be was courting the deceased, who stayed at his sister's house on the Tues- day night and at his father's house on Wednesday night. He had no quarrel with her, and there was no un- pleasanttiess between them.—The Coroner Then if she told her friends that she had quarreled with you, is it imagination?—Witness : Yes.—The Coroner : Well, you know what it means: I cannot imagine anyone taking away her life for your sake. Why don't you own up to it P—A Juror : It seems very strange.— Witness, continuing, said deceased had made arrange- ments to come back to the house on Sunday.—Peter Clarke,a youth ,poke to hearing screams from the direc- tion of the canal, and giving information to Constable Gloave, who said he recovered the body.—Mr. Broom- head, at whose house deceased had been engaged as domestic rervant, said she was a good girl, but highly sensitive and basily excited.—The jury returned a ver- alict of "Suicide whilst of unsound mind," and the Coroner said he did not think any blame attached to Dewney after they had heard that the girl was of a highly-4trung temperament. It would not be fair for the puhlic to think that he was either legally or morally responsible for the sad affair.

LOCAL MARRIAGE.

DAVIES-TINSLEY.

A marriage of great local interest was celebrated on Tuesday at All Maints' Charch, Darembury. The contracting parties were Mr. Edward Gaskell Davies, second son of Mr. Robert Davies, of Birch- dale, Stockton Heath, and Miss Mabel Harrison Tinsley, second daughter of Mr. James Tinsley, of Daresbury Hall, Moore. The church was crowded with a fashionable attendance long before the time fixed for the ceremony (2 o'clock) and the covered way leading to the church door was lined with spectators. Mr. John Wright, who presided at the organ, ren- dered the following selections before the ceremony in excellent style :—" Offertoire " (Felix Thorei), " March" (Arthur Page). The service was fully choral and of an impressive character. On the entrance of the wedding party into the church the hymn " The voice that breathed o"er Eden" was sung and "0 perfect love all human joy transcending" was also rendered during the course of the service. The Rev Canon Greenall, rector of Grappenhall, performed the marriage ceremony as- sisted by the Rev. H. T. Clark, vicar, and Rev. E. Charley, vicar of lace, near Chester. Mr. Leonard G. Davies, J.P. brother of the bridegroom was the best man and the bridesmaids were Miss Thomas (Frodsham), Mies Thornely and Miss Amy Whitley. The bride was given away by her father and was exquisitely attired in duchess@ satin trimmed with Irish lace (the gift of her sister), with court train. She carried a shower bouquet with white heather and cream roses,and wore pearls and diamond stars and gold bangles the gifts of the bridegroom. The bridesmaids wore handsome dresses of cream satin, trimmed with cream insertion and chiffon, with blue hats and carried pink bouquets tied with pale blue ribbons. They 5153 wore gold bangles set with turquoises and pearls the gifts of the bridegroom. At the conclusion of the servide the organist played "March " (Theophilus Bonheur),"March" (Newell), and Mendelssohn's " Wedding March " with great effect. Although the weather had been threaten- ing previous to the ceremony, the sun appeared during the service and when the party came out of church it was shining with much force. The bride's travelling dress was of grey viola trimmed with orange and white toque to match. Tae honeymoon will be spent in Scotland. After the ceremony a reception was held at the house of the bride's father.

PAROCHIAL COMMITTEE.—At the monthly meeting of the Committee, held on Friday evening, Mr. Charles E. Linaker presiding, the Clerk (Mr. Ashton) reported the receipt of letters from the Railway Company in reply to the complaint of the Committea relative to the consign- ments of bones, .3cc., which it had been alleged were allowed to remain at Fradsham Station too long. The Cerapany stated that only one waggon had been so allowed, as the Company did all in their power to pre- vent complaint.—Mr. Williams asked the Committee to allow the ^notim for the adoption ef the Town's 'folic At S:ALldirliZ its ais name to b 3 postponed to the nex meeting, as the clauses comprised in the Act he found very nuneerous.—The required per- mission was granted.—The Chairman ot the Light- ing Committee (Mr. Shore) produced a letter he had received from the Frodsham Gas and IN ater Company undertaking to light the street lamps, from the 16th inst. to the 15th May next, for toe sum of 30s per lamp. He explained that he did not press the com- pany to reduce the price per lamp, as during the past five years he had obtained reductions to the extent of 71 6d per lamp. The Surveyor introduced the subject of additional wages to the road men for cleaning out the manholes of the sewers, and after some consideration it was resolved that an extra 6,21 per day be granted.—Mr. Williams inquired whether the samples of water from the mains had been submitted for analysis—The In- spector stated the matter should have his immediate attention.—For the information of the Committee the Chairman stated that the report of the Jubilee celebration had been received from the Hon. Secretary (Mr- S. Daviei), and he hoped that a oopy report would be in the haads of the subscribers before the next meeting.

SAD FATALITY AT STOCKPORT. A GIRL JUMPS FROM A WINDOW:

Shortly before Wednesday midnight a sad incident occurred in High-street, Stockport, by which a girl, named Mary Ellen Arrandale, agei 18 year 3, lost her life. It appears from the statement math by the girl's father, that shortly after eleven o'clock his daughter came in the bouse, and he therEupon remonstrated with her, and told her that she would net have to keep late hours again. The girl ran upstairs, and shortly after- wards the window was raised, and the girl jumped through the window and fell with a thud on the pave- ment. The police were called in, ana it was then found that death had been almost instantaneaus,the aeck having been broken.

GREAT JEWEL ROBBERY. BIRMINGHAM TRAVELLER ROSBED IN LIVERPOOL.

A daring robbery of jowellery, iu which the thief got clear with £2,000 wortn, has just come to light at Liver- pool. On Tuesday night a Birmingham commercial traveller, Mr. Fred Burgess, representative of Messrs. Henry Griffiths and Sons, manufacturing j -wellers• called at the telegraph office in Lyme-street Station, to send a message away, and whilst his attention was momentarily diverted from a bag of jewellery on which he had placed his foot as it lay on the floor, a swell thief cooly lifted it, and at once disappeared with the bag. SCENE AT THE DREYFUS TRIAL. M. BERTULUS DENOUNCED AS A "JUDAS."

A dramatic scene followed the conclusion -of 111:13—ertu- lUeS statements yesterday,at the Dreyfus trial. Madame Henry entered the witness bcx' and standing beside the magistrate, described how she had warned her husband against the apparent friendliness of M. Bertulus. Amid great excitement she apostrophised the magistrate in open court as a " _Judas."

M. Bertulue said be would not reply to L lady who was seeking to defend her husband's memory and her child.

After this came Col. Picquart, who protested against the accusation of having made away with any docu- ments or having sought to substitute another officer for Dreyfus. Witness maintained that there was no evidence of treason on the part of Dreyfus txcept the bordereau, which, he submitted, being dated October, could not possibly have emanated from Dreyfus.
